Finally, we recognize the boot of Italy, visible from Mars, and the black spot marks the locality threatened, according to their calculation, by one of the most dangerous fragments of the head of the comet; while the four arrows radiating in the direction of the four cardinal points of the compass seem to counsel removal from the point menaced.

The photophonic message from the Martians was much longer and far more complicated. The astronomers on Mt. Gaurisankar had previously received several such, and had discovered that they were sent from a very important, intellectual and scientific center situated in the equatorial zone not far from Meridian bay. The last message, whose general meaning is given above, was the most important. The remainder of it had not been transmitted, as it was obscure and it was not certain that its exact meaning had been made out.

===Chinese===
“恐怕学会这些可敬的博士们，是一场天大玩笑的受害者吧。谁也别想让我相信，火星的居民——姑且承认他们存在，也姑且承认他们真的给我们发来了警告——居然知道意大利这个名字。我很怀疑他们中间有谁听说过《恺撒战记》或者《教皇史》，何况——”

这位正要乘兴发表一通有趣狂想的演说家，说到这里，被骤然关掉的电灯镇住了。除了天花板上那块发光的方板，全场陷入一片黑暗；那个声音又补了六个字：“这是火星发来的电文；”随即，可视电话的屏幕上出现了如下符号：

由于这幅图像只有把头抬到一个非常吃力的姿势才能看到，主席按了按铃，一名助手走上前来，借助一台投影器和一面镜子，把这些符号图形转投到讲席后方墙面的幕布上，让人人都能从容不迫地观看和分析。它们的解释很容易；的确，再没有比这更简单的了。代表彗星的那个图形无需任何说明。箭头标示着彗星朝一个天体的运动；这个天体从火星上看去呈现位相，像恒星一样闪闪发光；这自然指的是地球，是火星人按他们所见的模样画出来的——他们的眼睛在一种比我们的环境亮度较低的介质中发育，多少更敏锐一些，能分辨出地球的位相，何况他们的大气更稀薄、更透明，看起来也就更加容易。（对我们来说，金星的位相恰恰处在可见性的极限上。）那双重圆球代表正望着凯撒海的火星——那是火星地理最典型的特征——并表明彗星的速度是轨道速度的两倍或略低一些，因为那条线并没有完全画到边缘。火焰表示运动转化为热；随后的极光和闪电，表示向电力与磁力的转化。最后，我们认出了意大利那只靴子——从火星上望得见的——那黑点标记着，按照他们的计算，彗星头部最危险的碎片之一所威胁的地点；而朝着罗盘四个基点方向放射出去的四支箭，似乎是在劝告人们撤离受威胁的地点。

火星人的光声讯息比这长得多，也复杂得多。高里三卡山上的天文学家们此前已经收到过好几份，并发现它们发自赤道带上子午湾（Meridian bay）附近一个非常重要的、智慧而科学的中心。最后这一份——其大意已如上述——是头等重要的一份。其余的部分没有传输过来，因为它晦涩难解，其确切的含义是否已经被弄清，尚无把握。

===Original===
The president rang his bell for order. He was about to sum up what had been said, before adjourning the meeting.

“Gentlemen,” he began, “although it is after midnight, it will be of interest, before we separate, to summarize what has been told us in these two solemn sessions.

“The last despatch from Gaurisankar may well impress you. It seems clear that the inhabitants of Mars are farther advanced in science than ourselves, and this is not surprising, for they are a far older race and have had centuries innumerable in which to achieve this progress. Moreover, they may be much more highly organized than we are, they may possess better eyes, instruments of greater perfection, and intellectual faculties of a higher order. We observe, too, that their calculations, while in accord with ours as to the collision, are more precise, for they designate the very point which is to receive the greatest shock. The advice to flee from Italy should therefore be followed, and I shall at once telephone the Pope, who at this very moment is assembling the prelates of entire Christendom.

===Chinese===
主席摇铃要求安静。他准备在宣布散会之前，把大家的发言归纳一下。

“诸位先生，”他开口说道，“虽然已经过了午夜，但在我们分手之前，把这两次庄严会议中听到的内容总结一下，想必不无意义。

“高里三卡山发来的最后一份电文，或许会给诸位留下深刻的印象。火星的居民在科学上显然比我们先进，这并不奇怪，因为他们是一个古老得多的种族，拥有无数个世纪来完成这样的进步。况且，他们的机体组织也许比我们完善得多，也许拥有更好的眼睛、更精良的仪器、更高等级的智力官能。我们还注意到，他们的计算虽然在碰撞这一点上与我们的推算一致，却更为精确，因为他们指明了将遭受最大冲击的那一个点。因此，远离意大利的忠告应当听从；我这就打电话给教皇——此刻他正在召集整个基督教世界的高级教士。

===Original===
“So the comet will collide with the Earth, and no one can yet foresee the consequences. But in all probability the disturbance will be local and the world will not be destroyed. The carbonic-oxide is not likely to penetrate the respirable portions of the atmosphere, but there will be an enormous development of heat.

“As to the veritable end of the world, of all the hypotheses which today permit us to forecast that event the most probable is the last﻿—that explained to us by the learned chancellor of the Columbian academy: the life of the planet depends upon the Sun; so long as the Sun shines humanity is safe, unless indeed the diminution of the atmosphere and aqueous vapor should usher in before that time the reign of cold. In the former case we have yet before us twenty million years of life; in the latter only ten.

“Let us then await the night of July 13﻿–﻿14 without despair. I advise those who can to pass these fête days in Chicago, or better still in San Francisco, Honolulu or Noumea. The transatlantic electric airships are so numerous and well managed that millions of travellers may make the journey before Saturday night.”

===Chinese===
“所以，彗星将与地球相撞，而后果如何，尚无人能够预见。但极有可能，扰动将是局部性的，世界不会被毁灭。一氧化碳大概不会渗入大气中可供呼吸的部分，但将出现一场巨大的热爆发。

“至于世界真正的终结，在今天允许我们预见此事的所有假说之中，最可能的是最后一个——哥伦比亚科学院那位博学的院长向我们解释的那个：行星的生命依赖于太阳；只要太阳还在发光，人类就是安全的，除非大气与水汽的减少在那之前先引来严寒的统治。前一种情形，我们面前还有两千万年的生命；后一种情形，就只有一千万年了。

“那么，让我们不必怀着绝望去等待 7 月 13 日至 14 日的夜晚吧。我建议有条件的人到芝加哥去度过这几个节日，或者更好一些，去旧金山、檀香山或努美阿。跨大西洋的电动飞艇数量众多、管理完善，数以百万计的旅客可以在星期六夜里之前完成这趟旅程。”

## Part I / 第一部

## Part II / 第二部

### V / V

===Original===
While the above scientific discussions were taking place at Paris, meetings of a similar character were being held at London, Chicago, St. Petersburg, Yokohama, Melbourne, New York, and in all the principal cities of the world, in which every effort was made to throw light upon the great problem which so universally preoccupied the attention of humanity. At Oxford a theological council of the Reformed church was convened, in which religious traditions and interpretations were discussed at great length. To recite, or even to summarize here the proceedings of all these congresses would be an endless task, but we cannot omit reference to that of the Vatican as the most important from a religious point of view, just as that of the Institute of Paris was from a scientific one.

The council had been divided into a certain number of sections or committees, and the then often discussed question of the end of the world had been referred to one of these committees. Our duty here is to reproduce as accurately as possible the physiognomy of the main session, devoted to the discussion of this problem.

The patriarch of Jerusalem, a man of great piety and profound faith, was the first to speak in Latin. “Venerable fathers,” he began, “I cannot do better than to open before you the Holy Gospel. Permit me to quote literally.” He then read the words of the evangelists3 describing the last days of the Earth, and went on:

===Chinese===
当上述科学讨论正在巴黎进行之时，伦敦、芝加哥、圣彼得堡、横滨、墨尔本、纽约以及世界上所有的主要城市，也在召开性质相似的会议，千方百计地阐明那个普遍占据着人类心神的大问题。在牛津，改革宗召开了一次神学会议，会上对宗教传统和释经问题作了极为冗长的讨论。要在这里复述、哪怕只是概述所有这些会议的经过，都将是一件没完没了的工作；但我们不能不提到梵蒂冈的那一次——正如巴黎的法兰西学会会议从科学观点看最为重要一样，它从宗教观点看最为重要。

这次会议分为若干分组或委员会，而当时屡屡被人讨论的世界末日问题，被提交给了其中一个委员会。我们在这里的职责，是尽可能准确地再现专门讨论这一问题的全体大会的面貌。

耶路撒冷牧首（patriarch of Jerusalem）——一位极为虔诚、信仰深厚的人——第一个用拉丁语发言。“可敬的教父们，”他开口说，“我最好的做法，莫过于当着你们的面翻开神圣的福音书。请允许我逐字引用。”随后他朗读了福音书作者[3]描述地球最后日子的那些话，接着说道：

===Original===
“These words are taken verbatim from the Gospels, and you know that on this point the evangelists are in perfect accord.

“You also know, most reverend fathers, that the last great day is pictured in still more striking language in the Apocalypse of St. John. But every word of the Scriptures is known to you, and, in the presence of so learned an audience, it seems to me superfluous, if not out of place, to make further citations from what is upon every lip.”

Such was the beginning of the address of the patriarch of Jerusalem. His remarks were divided under three heads: First, the teachings of Christ; second, the traditions of the Church; third, the dogma of the resurrection of the body, and of the last judgment. Taking first the form of an historical statement, the address soon became a sort of sermon, of vast range; and when the orator, passing from St. Paul to Clement of Alexandria, Tertulian and Origen, reached the council of Nice and the dogma of universal resurrection, he was carried away by his subject in such a flight of eloquence as to move the heart of every prelate before him. Several, who had renounced the apostolic faith of the earlier centuries, felt themselves again under its spell. It must be said that the surroundings lent themselves marvellously to the occasion. The assembly took place in the Sistine chapel. The immense and imposing painting of Michelangelo, like a new apocalyptic heaven, was before every eye. The awful mingling of bodies, arms and legs, so forcibly and strangely foreshortened; Christ, the judge of the world; the damned borne struggling away by hideous devils; the dead issuing from their tombs; the skeletons returning to life and reclothing themselves with flesh; the frightful terror of humanity trembling in the presence of the wrath of God﻿—all seemed to give a vividness, a reality, to the magnificent periods of the patriarch’s oratory, and at times, in certain effects of light, one might almost hear the advancing trumpet sounding from heaven the call of judgment, and see between earth and sky the moving hosts of the resurrection.

===Chinese===
“这些话是一字不差地从四福音书里摘引的；你们知道，在这一点上，各位福音书作者完全一致。

“你们也知道，最可敬的教父们，最后那个大日子，在圣约翰的《启示录》里是用更加触目的语言描绘的。但圣经的每一句话你们都烂熟于心；面对这样博学的听众，再从人人挂在嘴边的段落摘引下去，在我看来即使不是不合时宜，至少也是多余。”

耶路撒冷牧首的讲话就是这样开的头。他的论述分为三个部分：第一，基督的教训；第二，教会的传统；第三，肉身复活与最后审判的信条。这段讲话起初采取历史陈述的形式，很快便变成了一场包罗宏富的布道；当演讲人从圣保罗讲到亚历山大的克莱门特、德尔图良和奥利金，再讲到尼西亚公会议和普遍复活的信条时，他被自己的题目带到了那样一种雄辩的高飞之中，震动了面前每一位主教的心。有几位早已背弃了前几个世纪的使徒信仰，此刻又感到自己重新受到它的魔力支配。必须说，周围的环境为这个场合提供了绝妙的助力。大会在西斯廷礼拜堂举行。米开朗琪罗那幅宏大而摄人的壁画，如同一片新的启示录天空，悬在每个人的眼前。肢体、手臂与大腿骇人地交织，透视短缩得那样有力而奇特；基督，世界的审判者；被狰狞的魔鬼拖拽着挣扎而去的不信者；从坟墓中走出的死人；重新获得生命、重新披上血肉的骸骨；在上帝的震怒面前战栗的人类的可怕恐怖——这一切，似乎都给牧首雄辩中华丽的句段增添了生动与真实；在某些光线的效果之下，人们几乎能听见审判的号角从天上遥遥推进，看见天地之间复活大军的移动。

===Original===
Scarcely had the patriarch of Jerusalem finished his speech, when an independent bishop, one of the most ardent dissenters of the council, the learned Mayerstross, rushed to the tribune, and began to insist that nothing in the Gospel, or the traditions of the Church, should be taken literally.

“The letter kills,” he cried, “the spirit vivifies! Everything is subject to the law of progress and change. The world moves. Enlightened Christians cannot any longer admit the resurrection of the body. All these images,” he added, “were good for the days of the catacombs. For a long time no one has believed in them. Such ideas are opposed to science, and, most reverend fathers you know, as well as I do, that we must be in accord with science, which has ceased to be, as in the time of Galileo, the humble servant of theology: *theologiae humilis ancilla*.

“The body cannot be reconstituted, even by a miracle, so long as its molecules return to nature and are appropriated, successively, by so many beings﻿—human, animal and vegetable. We are formed of the dust of the dead, and, in the future, the molecules of oxygen, hydrogen, nitrogen, carbon, phosphorus, sulphur, or iron, which make up our flesh and our bones, will be incorporated in other human organisms. This change is perpetual, even during life. One human being dies every second; that is more than 86,000 each day, more than 30,000,000 each year, more than three milliards each century. In a hundred centuries﻿—not a long period in the history of a planet, the number of the resurrected would be three hundred milliards. If the human race lived but a 100,000 years﻿—and no one here is ignorant of the fact that geological and astronomical periods are estimated by millions of years﻿—there would be gathered before the judgment throne something like three thousand milliards of men, women and children. My estimate is a modest one, because I take no account of the secular increase in population. You may reply to me, that only the saved will rise! What, then, will become of the others? Two weights and two measures! Death and life! Night and day, good and evil! Divine injustice and goodwill, reigning together over creation! But, no, you will not accept such a solution. The eternal law is the same for all. Well! What will you do with these thousands of milliards? Show me the valley of Jehoshaphat vast enough to contain them. Will you spread them over the surface of the globe, do away with the oceans and the icefields of the poles, and cover the world with a forest of human bodies? So be it! And afterwards? What will become of this immense host? No, most holy fathers, our beliefs must not, cannot, be taken literally. Would that there were here no theologians with closed eyes, that look only within, but astronomers with open eyes, that look without.”

===Chinese===
耶路撒冷牧首的话音刚落，一位独立的会督——会议上最激烈的异见者之一、博学的迈耶斯特罗斯（Mayerstross）——便冲上讲坛，开始力主福音书或教会传统中的任何内容都不应按字面理解。

“字句叫人死，”他喊道，“精神叫人活！一切都服从进步与变化的法则。世界在运动。开明的基督徒再也不能承认肉身的复活了。所有这些形象，”他补充说，“只适合地下墓穴的时代。很久以来就没有人相信它们了。这样的观念与科学对立；而最可敬的教父们，你们和我一样清楚：我们必须与科学保持一致——科学早已不再像伽利略时代那样，作神学谦卑的婢女：*theologiae humilis ancilla*。”

“肉身无法重新组成，纵有奇迹也不行，因为它的分子一回归自然，便会陆续被那么多的存在——人的、动物的、植物的——取用。我们由死者的尘埃构成；将来，构成我们血肉与骨骼的氧、氢、氮、碳、磷、硫或铁的分子，将被纳入别的人体。这种变化永不停歇，即使在活着的时候也是如此。每秒钟死去一个人；也就是说，每天超过 86,000 人，每年超过 30,000,000 人，每世纪超过三十亿人。一百个世纪——这在行星的历史上不算一段漫长的时期——复活者的数目就将是三千亿。倘若人类只存在 100,000 年——而这里没有人不知道，地质纪元和天文纪元都是以百万年来估算的——那么聚在审判宝座前的，就会有大约三万亿的男人、女人和儿童。我这个估计还是保守的，因为我没有把人口的长期增长计算在内。你们或许会回答我：只有得救的人才会复活！那么，其余的人怎么办？双重标准！死与生！夜与昼，善与恶！神圣的不公与善意，一同君临造物！不，你们不会接受这样的解决方案。永恒的法则对所有人一视同仁。好啊！你们拿这几万亿人怎么办？给我指出一个宽敞得容得下他们的约沙法谷。你们要把他们摊满地球的表面、取消海洋和两极的冰原、让世界覆盖上一片人体的森林吗？就算这样吧！然后呢？这支浩浩荡荡的大军将来会怎样？不，最神圣的教父们，我们的信仰不该、也不能按字面理解。但愿在这里的不是些闭着眼睛、只向内看的神学家，而是些睁着眼睛、向外看的天文学家。”

===Original===
These words had been uttered in the midst of an indescribable tumult; several times they wished to silence the Croatian bishop, gesticulating violently and denouncing him as schismatic; but the rules did not permit this, for the greatest liberty was allowed in the discussion. An Irish cardinal called down upon him the thunders of the Church, and spoke of excommunication and anathema; then, a distinguished prelate of the Gallican church, no less a person than the archbishop of Paris himself, ascended the rostrum and declared that the dogma of the resurrection of the dead might be discussed without incurring any canonical blame, and that it might be interpreted in entire harmony with reason and faith. According to him one might admit the dogma, and at the same time recognize the rational impossibility of a resurrection of the body!

“The Doctor Angelicus,” he said, speaking of St. Thomas, “maintained that the complete dissolution of every human body by fire would take place before the resurrection. (*Summa theologica*, III.) I readily concede with Calmet (on the resurrection of the dead) that to the omnipotence of the Creator it would not be impossible to reassemble the scattered molecules in such a way that the resurrected body should not contain a single one which did not belong to it at some time during its mortal life. But such a miracle is not necessary. St. Thomas has himself shown (*loco citato*) that this complete material identity is by no means indispensable to establish the perfect identity of the resurrected body with the body destroyed by death. I also think, therefore, that the letter should give way to the spirit.

“What is the principle of identity in a living body? Assuredly it does not consist in the complete and persistent identity of its *matter*. For in this continual change and renewal, which is the very essence of physiological life, the elements, which have belonged successively from infancy to old age to the same human being, would form a colossal body. In this torrent of life the elements pass and change ceaselessly; but the organism remains the same, notwithstanding the modifications in its size, its form and its constitution. Does the growing stem of the oak, hidden between its two cotyledons, cease to be the same plant when it has become a mighty oak? Is the embryo of the caterpillar, while yet in the egg, no longer the same insect when it becomes a caterpillar, and then a chrysalis, and then a butterfly? Is individuality lost as the child passes through manhood to old age? Assuredly not. But in the case of the oak, the butterfly, and the man, is there a single remaining molecule of those which constituted the growing stem of the oak, the egg of the caterpillar or the human embryo? What then is the principle which persists through all these changes? This principle is a reality, not a fiction. It is not the soul, for the plants have life, and yet no souls, in the meaning of the word as we use it. Nevertheless, it must be an imponderable agent. Does it survive the body? It is possible. St. Gregory of Nyssus believed so. If it remains united to the soul, it may be invoked to furnish it with a new body identical with that which death has destroyed, even though this body should not possess *a single molecule* which it possessed at any period of its terrestrial life, and this would be as truly our body as that which we had when five, fifteen, or thirty, or sixty years of age.

===Chinese===
这些话是在难以形容的骚乱中说出来的；有好几次，人们想让这位手舞足蹈、被斥为分裂派的克罗地亚主教闭嘴；但议事规则不允许这样做，因为辩论享有最大的自由。一位爱尔兰红衣主教把教会的雷霆降到了他的头上，大谈绝罚与咒诅；随后，高卢教会一位显赫的高级教士——不是别人，正是巴黎大主教本人——登上讲坛，宣称死者复活的信条可以在不招致任何教会法谴责的情况下加以讨论，而且可以对它作出与理性和信仰完全调和的解释。照他的说法，人们尽可以承认这条信条，同时又承认肉身复活在理性上的不可能！

“天使博士，”他谈到圣托马斯时说道，“主张每一个人的身体都会在复活之前先被火完全消解。（《神学大全》，第三卷。）我乐于随同卡尔梅特（论死者复活）承认：对造物主的全能来说，把散落的分子重新聚拢，使复活的身体不含一个不曾在此人可朽生命的某个时刻属于过它的分子，并不是不可能的。但这样的奇迹并非必需。圣托马斯本人已经表明（出处同前），这种完全的物质同一性，对于确立复活之身与被死亡毁坏之身的完全同一，绝非不可缺少。因此我也认为，字句应当让位于精神。”

“一个活体的同一性原则是什么？可以肯定，它不在于其*物质*完全而持续的同一。因为在这种持续的变化与更新之中——这恰恰是生理生命的本质——那些从婴儿到老年陆续属于同一个人的元素，会组成一个庞大的躯体。在这股生命的激流中，元素不断流过、不断改变；而机体始终是同一个机体，尽管它的大小、形态和构造发生了种种改变。橡树那棵藏在两片子叶之间的生长的嫩茎，长成一株参天橡树之后，就不再是同一株植物了吗？毛虫的胚胎，还在卵中的时候，变成毛虫、再变成蛹、再变成蝴蝶，就不再是同一只昆虫了吗？人从童年经过壮年走向老年，个性就丢失了吗？肯定没有。但是，就橡树、蝴蝶和人而言，那些曾构成橡树嫩茎、毛虫之卵或人类胚胎的分子，还剩下哪怕一个吗？那么，贯穿这一切变化而持存的原则是什么？这个原则是一种实在，不是一种虚构。它不是灵魂，因为植物有生命，却在我们使用这个词的意义上没有灵魂。然而，它必定是一种不可称量的能动本原。它能在死后继续存在吗？这是可能的。尼撒的格列高利就是这样相信的。如果它始终与灵魂相结合，便可以请它为灵魂提供一个与被死亡毁坏的身体同一的新身体，纵使这个身体不曾拥有其尘世生命任何时期具有过的*哪怕一个分子*；而这个身体，将和我们 5 岁、15 岁、30 岁或 60 岁时的身体一样，真正是我们自己的身体。”

===Original===
“Such a conception agrees perfectly with the expressions of holy writ, according to which it is certain that after a period of separation the soul will again take on the body forever.

“In addition to St. Gregory of Nyssus, permit me, most reverend fathers, to cite a philosopher Leibnitz, who held the opinion that the physiological principle of life was imponderable but not incorporeal, and that the soul remains united to this principle, although separated from the ponderable and visible body. I do not pretend to either accept or reject this hypothesis. I only note that it may serve to explain the dogma of the resurrection, in which every Christian should firmly believe.”

“This effort to conciliate reason and faith,” interrupted the Croatian bishop, “is worthy of praise, but it seems to me more ingenious than probable. Are these bodies, bodies like our own? If they are perfect, incorruptible, fitted to their new conditions, they must not possess any organ for which there is no use. Why a mouth, if they do not eat? Why legs, if they do not walk? Why arms, if they do not work? One of the fathers of the early church, Origen, whose personal sacrifice is not forgotten, thought these bodies must be perfect spheres. That would be logical but not very beautiful or interesting.”

===Chinese===
“这样一个概念，与圣书的说法完全吻合；按照圣书，可以确定：经过一段分离的时期之后，灵魂将重新接取身体，直到永远。

“除了尼撒的格列高利，请容许我，最可敬的教父们，再引一位哲学家莱布尼茨；他持有这样的见解：生命的生理本原不可称量，但并非无形无体；灵魂始终与这个本原相结合，尽管与那可称量的、可见的身体相分离。我并不自命要接受或拒绝这个假说。我只是指出：它可以为复活的信条服务——每一个基督徒都应当坚信这条信条。”

“这种调和理性与信仰的努力，”克罗地亚主教插话道，“值得称道；但在我看来，机巧有余，可靠不足。这些身体，是像我们这样的身体吗？倘若它们是完美的、不会朽坏的、适配于新的处境的，它们就不该拥有任何用不着的器官。既然不吃，何必有嘴？既然不走，何必有腿？既然不劳作，何必有手臂？早期教会的一位教父，奥利金——他自我牺牲的事迹并未被人遗忘——认为这些身体必定是完美的球体。那样固然合乎逻辑，却不怎么美观，也不怎么有趣。”

===Original===
“It is better to admit with St. Gregory of Nyssus and St. Augustin,” replied the archbishop, “that the resurrected body will have the human form, a transparent veil of the beauty of the soul.”

Thus was the modern theory of the Church on the resurrection of the body summed up by the French cardinal. As to the objections on the score of the locality of the resurrection, the number of the resurrected, the insufficiency of surface on the globe, the final abode of the elect and the damned, it was impossible to come to any common understanding for the contradictions were irreconcilable. The resultant impression was, however, that these matters also should be understood figuratively, that neither the heaven or the hell of the theologian represented any definite place, but rather states of the soul, of happiness or of misery, and that life, whatever its form, would be perpetuated on the countless worlds which people infinite space. And so it appeared that Christian thought had gradually become transformed, among the enlightened, and followed the progress of astronomy and the other sciences.

The council had been held on Tuesday evening, that is to say on the day following the two meetings of the Institute, of which an account has been given above. The Pope had made public the advice of the president of the Institute to leave Italy on the fatal day, but no attention had been paid to it, partly because death is a deliverance for every believer, and partly because most theologians denied the existence even of inhabitants upon Mars.

===Chinese===
“还是承认——随同尼撒的格列高利和圣奥古斯丁，”大主教回答说，“复活之身将具有人的形貌，是灵魂之美的一层透明的面纱，这样更好。”

法兰西红衣主教对教会关于肉身复活的现代理论所作的概括便是如此。至于针对复活的地点、复活的人数、地球表面的不足、选民与被打入地狱者的最终居所等等提出的异议，则不可能达成任何一致的意见，因为那些矛盾无法调和。然而，人们得到的印象是：这些事情也应当按寓意来理解；神学家的天堂和地狱并不代表任何确定的处所，而毋宁代表灵魂的状态——幸福的状态或悲惨的状态；而生命，无论采取什么形式，都将在人居于无限空间的无数世界上延续下去。于是事情显得：在开明人士中间，基督教思想已经逐渐发生了转变，追随着天文学和其他科学的进步。

这次会议是在星期二晚上举行的，也就是上文所述法兰西学会那两次会议的次日。教皇已经把学会主席关于在那个致命之日离开意大利的忠告公之于众，但没有人把它放在心上：一半因为死亡对每一个信徒而言是一种解脱，一半因为大多数神学家连火星上有居民这件事都不肯承认。

### VI / VI

===Original===
It is now time to pause, amid the eventful scenes through which we are passing, in order to consider this new fear of the end of the world with others which have preceded it, and to pass rapidly in review the remarkable history of this idea, which has reappeared again and again in the past. At the time of which we are speaking, this subject was the sole theme of conversation in every land and in every tongue.

As to the dogma “*Credo Resurrectionem Carnis*,” the addresses of the fathers of the Church before the council assembled in the Sistine chapel at Rome, were, on the whole, in accord with the opinion expressed by the cardinal archbishop of Paris. The clause “*et vitam aeternam*” was tacitly ignored, in view of the possible discoveries of astronomy and psychology. These addresses epitomized, as it were, the history of the doctrine of the end of the world as held by the Christian Church in all ages.

This history is interesting, for it is also the history of the human mind face to face with its own destiny, and we believe it of sufficient importance to devote to it a separate chapter. For the time being, therefore, we abandon our role as the chronicler of the twenty-fifth century, and return to our own times, in order to consider this doctrine from an historical point of view.

===Chinese===
现在正是时候，让我们在身历其境的这一连串大事之中停顿片刻，把这场新的世界末日之恐与此前的种种同类恐惧作一番比较，并匆匆回顾一下这个观念引人瞩目的历史——它在过去曾一再重现。在我们所说的那个时代，这个题目是每一片土地、每一种语言中唯一的话题。

至于「*Credo Resurrectionem Carnis*」（我信肉身复活）这条信条，聚在罗马西斯廷礼拜堂的教父们在会议上的发言，总体上与巴黎红衣大主教表达的意见相合。「*et vitam aeternam*」（及永生）一句则被心照不宣地搁置一旁，因为顾及天文学与心理学可能的发现。这些发言可以说集中概括了基督教会历世历代所持世界末日学说的历史。

这段历史饶有兴味，因为它也是人类心智直面自身命运的历史；我们相信它足够重要，值得专门用一章来讲述。因此，眼下我们暂且搁下二十五世纪纪事者的角色，回到我们自己的时代，从历史的观点来考察这一学说。

===Original===
The existence of a profound and tenacious faith is as old as the centuries, and it is a notable fact that all religions, irrespective of Christian dogma, have opened the same door from this mortal life upon the unknown which lies beyond, it is the door of the Divine Comedy of Dante, although the conceptions of paradise, hell and purgatory peculiar to the Christian Church, are not universal.

Zoroaster and the Zend-Avesta taught that the world would perish by fire. The same idea is found in the Epistle of St. Peter. It seems that the traditions of Noah and of Deucalion, according to which the first great disaster to humanity came by flood, indicated that the second great disaster would be of an exactly opposite character.

The apostles Peter and Paul died, probably, in the year 64, during the horrible slaughter ordered by Nero after the burning of Rome, which had been fired at his command and whose destruction he attributed to the Christians in order that he might have a pretext for new persecutions. St. John wrote the Apocalypse in the year 69. The reign of Nero was a bloody one, and martyrdom seemed to be the natural consequence of a virtuous life. Prodigies appeared on every hand; there were comets, falling stars, eclipses, showers of blood, monsters, earthquakes, famines, pestilences, and above all, there was the Jewish war and the destruction of Jerusalem. Never, perhaps, were so many horrors, so much cruelty and madness, so many catastrophes, crowded into so short a period as in the years 64﻿–﻿69 AD. The little church of Christ was apparently dispersed. It was impossible to remain in Jerusalem. The horrors of the reign of terror of 1793, and of the Commune of 1871, were as nothing in comparison with those of the Jewish civil war. The family of Jesus was obliged to leave the holy city and to seek safety in flight. False prophets appeared, thus verifying former prophecies. Vesuvius was preparing the terrible eruption of the year 79, and already, in 63, Pompeii had been destroyed by an earthquake.

===Chinese===
一种深厚而执着的信仰，其存在与诸世纪同古；而且一个值得注意的事实是：一切宗教，无论是否关乎基督教教义，都从这必朽的今生向那未知的彼岸敞开了同一扇门——那便是但丁《神曲》之门——尽管天堂、地狱和炼狱这些基督教会特有的观念，并非放之四海而皆准。

琐罗亚斯德（Zoroaster）和《阿维斯陀》教导说，世界将毁于火。同样的观念也见于圣彼得的书信。诺亚和丢卡利翁的传说——人类的第一次大灾由洪水而来——似乎表明，第二次大灾将具有恰好相反的性质。

使徒彼得和保罗大概死于公元 64 年，死于尼禄在罗马大火后下令进行的那场骇人听闻的屠杀——那场大火本是奉他的命令点燃的，他却把毁灭的罪责推到基督徒身上，好为自己制造新的迫害的借口。圣约翰在公元 69 年写下《启示录》。尼禄的统治血雨腥风，殉道似乎成了德行生活的自然结局。奇异的征兆到处出现：彗星、流星、日月食、血雨、怪物、地震、饥荒、瘟疫，而尤甚者，还有犹太战争和耶路撒冷的毁灭。或许从来不曾有过这么多恐怖、这么多残忍与疯狂、这么多灾祸，挤在像公元 64 至 69 年这样短促的一段时间里。基督的小小教会表面上已经四散。耶路撒冷是待不下去了。1793 年恐怖统治和 1871 年巴黎公社的种种惨状，若与犹太内战相比，简直不值一提。耶稣的家人不得不离开圣城，避祸远走。假先知纷纷出现，应验了从前的预言。维苏威火山正在酝酿公元 79 年那场可怕的喷发，而早在 63 年，庞贝已被一场地震摧毁。

===Original===
There was every indication that the end of the world was at hand. Nothing was wanting. The Apocalypse announced it.

But a calm followed the storm. The terrible Jewish war came to an end; Nero fell before Galba; under Vespasian and Titus, peace (71) succeeded war, and﻿—the end of the world was not yet.

Once more it became necessary to interpret anew the words of the evangelists. The coming of Christ was put off until after the fall of the Roman empire, and thus considerable margin was given to the commentator. A firm belief in a final and even an imminent catastrophe persisted, but it was couched in vague terms, which robbed the spirit as well as the letter of the prophecy of all precision. Still, the conviction remained.

St. Augustine devotes the XXth book of the *City of God* (426) to the regeneration of the world, the resurrection, the last judgment, and the New Jerusalem; in the XXIst book he describes the everlasting torments of hellfire. A witness to the fall of Rome and the empire, the bishop of Carthage believed these events to be the first act of the drama. But the reign of God was to continue a thousand years before the coming of Satan.

===Chinese===
一切迹象都表明世界末日近在眼前。什么都不缺了。《启示录》早已宣告。

然而风暴过后是平静。可怕的犹太战争结束了；尼禄在加尔巴面前倒了台；在韦斯巴芗和提图斯治下，和平（71 年）接替了战争，而——世界末日还没有来。

于是有必要把福音书作者的话重新解释一遍。基督的降临被推迟到罗马帝国倾覆之后，这样便给解经家留下了相当的余地。对一场最终的、甚至迫在眉睫的大灾的坚定信仰依然存在，只是措辞含混，使预言无论就精神还是字面都失去了全部的精确性。尽管如此，信念仍在。

圣奥古斯丁在《上帝之城》（426 年）第二十卷中论述世界的更新、复活、最后审判和新耶路撒冷；在第二十一卷里，他描绘地狱之火的无尽折磨。这位迦太基主教见证了罗马和帝国的陷落，相信这些事件只是这出戏剧的第一幕。但上帝之国还要延续一千年，撒但才会到来。

===Original===
St. Gregory, bishop of Tours (573), the first historian of the Franks, began his history as follows: “As I am about to relate the wars of the kings with hostile nations, I feel impelled to declare my belief. The terror with which men await the end of the world decides me to chronicle the years already passed, that thus one may know exactly how many have elapsed since the beginning of the world.”

This tradition was perpetuated from year to year and from century to century, notwithstanding that nature failed to confirm it. Every catastrophe, earthquake, epidemic, famine and flood, every phenomenon, eclipse, comet, storm, sudden darkness and tempest, was looked upon as the forerunner and herald of the final cataclysm. Trembling like leaves in the blast, the faithful awaited the coming judgment; and preachers successfully worked upon this dread apprehension, so deeply rooted in every heart.

But, as generation after generation passed, it became necessary to define again the widespread tradition, and about this time the idea of a millennium took form in the minds of commentators. There were many sects which believed that Christ would reign with the saints a thousand years before the day of judgment. St. Irenus, St. Papias, and St. Sulpicius Severus shared this belief, which acquired an exaggerated and sensual form in the minds of many, who looked forward to a day of general rejoicing for the elect and a reign of pleasure. St. Jerome and St. Augustine did much to discredit these views, but did not attack the central doctrine of a resurrection. Commentators on the Apocalypse continued to flourish through the somber night of the middle ages, and in the tenth century especially the belief gained ground that the year 1000 was to usher in the great change.

===Chinese===
图尔主教圣格列高利（573 年）——法兰克人的第一位历史学家——是这样开始他的历史的：“由于我即将讲述诸王与敌国作战的事迹，我必须申明我的信仰。人们对世界末日心怀恐惧，正是这种恐惧促使我把已经过去的岁月记录下来，好让人确切知道自世界肇始以来，已经流逝了多少年。”

这一传统年复一年、世纪复一世纪地延续着，尽管自然界并不肯给它作证。每一场灾祸、地震、时疫、饥荒和洪水，每一种现象、日食、彗星、风暴、骤然的昏暗和狂飙，都被视为最后大灾的前兆和通报。信徒们像疾风中的落叶一样战栗着等待将临的审判；布道者们则成功地撩拨着这种深深植根于每颗心灵的忧惧。

但是，一代又一代人过去了，有必要对这个流传广泛的传统重新加以界定；大约就在这个时期，千禧年的观念在解经家心中成形。有许多教派相信，基督将与众圣徒一同作王一千年，然后才是审判之日。圣爱任纽、圣帕皮亚和圣苏尔皮西乌斯·塞维鲁都持此信仰；它在许多人心里取得了一种夸张而耽于肉欲的形式，他们盼望的是选民普天同庆的一天和享乐的统治。圣哲罗姆和圣奥古斯丁大力贬抑这些看法，却没有触动复活这一核心信条。《启示录》的注释家们在中世纪沉沉的长夜里继续层出不穷，尤以十世纪为甚：公元 1000 年将迎来大变的信念愈传愈广。

===Original===
This conviction of an approaching end of the world, if not universal, was at least very general. Several charters of the period began with this sentence: *Termino mundi appropinquante*: “The end of the world drawing near.” In spite of some exceptions, it seems difficult not to share the opinion of historians, notably of Michelet, Henry Martin, Guizot, and Duruy, regarding the prevalence of this belief throughout Christendom. Doubtless, neither the French monk Gerbert, at that time Pope Sylvester II, nor King Robert of France, regulated their lives by their superstition, but it had none the less penetrated the conscience of the fainthearted, and many a sermon was preached from this text of the Apocalypse:

> “And when the thousand years are expired, Satan shall be loosed out of his prison, and shall go out to deceive the nations which are in the four quarters of the Earth﻿ ﻿… and another book was opened, which is the Book of Life﻿ ﻿… and the sea gave up the dead which were in it: and death and hell gave up the dead which were in them: and they were judged every man according to his works﻿ ﻿… and I saw a new heaven and a new earth.”

===Chinese===
这种世界临近终结的信念，即使不算普遍，至少也非常广泛。那个时期的好几份特许状都以这句话开头：*Termino mundi appropinquante*——“世界末日临近”。虽有一些例外，但似乎很难不赞同历史学家们——尤其是米什莱、亨利·马丁、基佐和迪吕伊——关于这一信仰遍及整个基督教世界的意见。毫无疑问，无论是那时身为教皇西尔维斯特二世的法国修士热尔贝，还是法兰西国王罗贝尔，都没有让自己的生活受制于这种迷信；但它毕竟已经渗入了怯懦者的良心，许多布道正是以《启示录》的这段经文为题：

> “那一千年完了，撒但必从监牢里被释放，出来要迷惑地上四方的列国……另有一卷展开，就是生命册……于是海交出其中的死人，死亡和阴间也交出其中的死人，他们都照各人所行的受审判……我又看见一个新天新地。”


===Original===
Bernard, a hermit of Thuringia, had taken these very words of Revelation as the text of his preaching, and in about the year 960 he publicly announced that the end of the world was at hand. He even fixed the fatal day itself, as that on which “The Annunciation” and Holy Friday should fall on the same day, a coincidence which really occurred in 992.

Druthmar, a monk of Corbie, prophesied the end of the world for the 24th of March in the year 1000. In many cities popular terror was so great on that day that the people sought refuge in the churches, remaining until midnight, prostrate before the relics of the saints, in order to await there the last trump and to die at the foot of the cross.

From this epoch date many gifts to the Church. Lands and goods were given to the monasteries. Indeed, an authentic and very curious document is preserved, written in the year 1000 by a certain monk, Raoul Glaber, on whose first pages we find: “Satan will soon be unloosed, as prophesied by St. John, *the thousand years having been accomplished*. It is of these years that we are to speak.”

===Chinese===
图林根的隐修士贝尔纳正是拿《启示录》里这些话作他布道的经文；约在公元 960 年，他公开宣布世界末日近在眼前。他甚至定下了那个致命的日子本身：即“圣母领报节”与神圣星期五落在同一天的那一天——这种巧合确实在 992 年出现过。

科尔比的修士德鲁特马尔预言，世界末日将在公元 1000 年 3 月 24 日来临。那天，许多城市的民众恐惧至极，纷纷躲进教堂，直到午夜还伏在圣徒的圣髑前，在那里等待最后的号角，渴望死在十字架的脚下。

教会从这个时期起收到了大量馈赠。土地和财物被捐给修道院。确实，有一份真实而十分奇特的文件保存了下来，写于公元 1000 年，出自一位名叫拉乌尔·格拉贝尔的修士之手；在开头几页上我们看到：“撒但即将被释放，正如圣约翰所预言的，*一千年已经满了*。我们要讲的正是这些年头。”

===Original===
The end of the tenth century and the beginning of the eleventh century was a truly strange and fearful period. From 980 to 1040 it seemed as if the angel of death had spread his wings over the world. Famine and pestilence desolated the length and breadth of Europe. There was in the first place the “*mal des ardents*,” the flesh of its victims decaying and falling from the bones, was consumed as by fire, and the members themselves were destroyed and fell away. Wretches thus afflicted thronged the roads leading to the shrines and besieged the churches, filling them with terrible odors, and dying before the relics of the saints. The fearful pest made more than forty thousand victims in Acquitania, and devastated the southern portions of France.

Then came famine, ravaging a large part of Christendom. Of the seventy-three years between 987 and 1060, forty-eight were years of famine and pestilence. The invasion of the Huns, between 910 and 945, revived the horrors of Attila, and the soil was so laid waste by wars between domains and provinces that it ceased to be cultivated. For three years rain fell continuously; it was impossible either to sow or to reap. The earth became barren and was abandoned. “The price of a ‘muid’ of wheat,” writes Raoul Glaber, “rose to sixty gold sous; the rich waxed thin and pale; the poor gnawed the roots of trees, and many were in such extremity as to devour human flesh. The strong fell upon the weak in the public highways, tore them in pieces, and roasted them for food. Children were enticed by an egg or some fruit into byways, where they were devoured. This frenzy of hunger was such that the beast was safer than man. Famished children killed their parents, and mothers feasted upon their children. One person exposed human flesh for sale in the market place of Tournus, as if it were a staple article of food. He did not deny the fact and was burned at the stake. Another, stealing this flesh by night from the spot where it had been buried, was also burned alive.”

This testimony is that of one who lived at the time and in many cases was an eye witness to what he relates. On every side people were perishing of hunger, and did not scruple to eat reptiles, unclean animals, and even human flesh. In the depths of the forest of Mâcon, in the vicinity of a church dedicated to St. John, a wretch had built a hut in which he strangled pilgrims and wayfarers. One day a traveller entering the hut with his wife to seek rest, saw in a corner the heads of men, women and children. Attempting to fly, they were prevented by their host. They succeeded, however, in escaping, and on reaching Mâcon, related what they had seen. Soldiers were sent to the bloody spot, where they counted forty-eight human heads. The murderer was dragged to the town and burned alive. The hut and the ashes of the funeral pile were seen by Raoul Glaber. So numerous were the corpses that burial was impossible, and disease followed close upon famine. Hordes of wolves preyed upon the unburied. Never before had such misery been known.

===Chinese===
十世纪末和十一世纪初是一个真正离奇而可怕的时期。从 980 年到 1040 年，死亡天使仿佛张开翅膀笼罩了世界。饥荒和瘟疫蹂躏了欧洲各地。首先是“*mal des ardents*”（圣火病），患者的皮肉溃烂脱骨，像被火烧灼一般地消耗，肢体本身也毁坏脱落。身染此病的可怜人挤满了通往圣殿的道路，围住教堂，使里面充满可怕的恶臭，在圣徒的圣髑前死去。这可怕的疫病在阿基坦夺去了四万多人的生命，蹂躏了法兰西南部。

接着是饥荒，蹂躏了基督教世界的大片土地。在 987 年至 1060 年之间的七十三年里，有四十八年是饥荒和瘟疫之年。910 年至 945 年间匈奴人的入侵，复活了阿提拉的恐怖；领地与行省之间的战争使土地惨遭荒废，再也无人耕种。一连三年，雨下个不停；播种和收割都不可能了。土地变得贫瘠，被人抛弃。“一‘缪伊’小麦的价钱，”拉乌尔·格拉贝尔写道，“涨到六十金苏；富人变得消瘦苍白；穷人啃嚼树根，许多人窘迫到吃人肉的地步。强者在大道上扑向弱者，把他们撕碎烤了吃。孩子们被一个鸡蛋或一点水果诱到僻巷里，在那里被吞食。饥饿的狂乱到了这步田地：牲畜比人还安全。饿极的孩子杀死父母，母亲以自己的孩子为盛宴。有人在图尔尼的市场上公开摆卖人肉，仿佛那是家常的食品。他不否认此事，被处以火刑。另一个人夜里去偷先前埋掉的人肉，也被活活烧死。”

这份证词出自一位亲历者之口，在许多情况下，他就是自己所述事件的目击者。四面八方都有人饿死，人们毫不迟疑地吃爬虫、不洁的动物，甚至人肉。在马孔森林深处、一座供奉圣约翰的教堂附近，有一个恶人盖了间小屋，在里面扼死朝圣者和过路人。一天，一个旅客带着妻子进屋歇脚，看见角落里堆着男人、女人和孩子的头颅。他们想逃，却被主人拦住；不过他们终究脱了身，一到马孔便讲述了所见的一切。士兵被派往那个血腥的地点，数出了四十八颗人头。凶手被拖进城，活活烧死。那间小屋和火刑堆的灰烬，拉乌尔·格拉贝尔都亲眼见过。尸体多得无法掩埋，瘟疫紧跟着饥荒而来。狼群大肆吞食那些无人埋葬的尸体。如此悲惨的境况前所未有。

===Original===
War and pillage were the universal rule, but these scourges from heaven made men somewhat more reasonable. The bishops came together, and it was agreed to establish a truce for four days of each week, from Wednesday night to Monday morning. This was known as the truce of God.

It is not strange that the end of so miserable a world was both the hope and the terror of this mournful period.

The year 1000, however, passed like its predecessors, and the world continued to exist. Were the prophets wrong again, or did the thousand years of Christendom point to the year 1033? The world waited and hoped. In that very year occurred a total eclipse of the Sun; “The great source of light became saffron colored; gazing into each other’s faces men saw that they were pale as death; every object presented a livid appearance; stupor seized upon every heart and a general catastrophe was expected.” But the end of the world was not yet.

===Chinese===
战争和劫掠是普遍的通则，但这些来自上天的天罚多少使人清醒了一些。主教们聚在一起，商定每周休战四天，从星期三夜晚到星期一早晨。这便是人所共知的“上帝的休战”。

这样悲惨的一个世界，它的终结既是这个凄凉年代的希望，也是它的恐怖，这不足为怪。

然而，公元 1000 年像之前的年份一样过去了，世界继续存在。是预言家们又错了呢，还是基督教的一千年指的是 1033 年？世界等待着，盼望着。就在那一年发生了一次日全食；“那伟大的光源变成了番红花色；人们面面相觑，看见彼此的脸苍白如死；每样东西都呈现一种青灰的颜色；惊愕攫住了每一颗心，人人以为大祸临头。”但世界末日还没有来。

===Original===
It was to this critical period that we owe the construction of the magnificent cathedrals which have survived the ravages of time and excited the wonder of centuries. Immense wealth had been lavished upon the clergy, and their riches increased by donations and inheritance. A new era seemed to be at hand. “After the year 1000,” continues Raoul Glaber, “the holy basilicas throughout the world were entirely renovated, especially in Italy and Gaul, although for the most part they were in no need of repair. Christian nations vied with each other in the erection of magnificent churches. It seemed as if the entire world, animated by a common impulse, shook off the rags of the past to put on a new garment; and the faithful were not content to rebuild nearly all the episcopal churches, but also embellished the monasteries dedicated to the various saints, and even the chapels in the smaller villages.”

The somber year 1000 had followed the vanished centuries into the past, but through what troubled times the Church had passed! The popes were the puppets of the rival Saxon emperors and the princes of Latium. All Christendom was in arms. The crisis had passed, but the problem of the end of the world remained, and credence in this dread event, though uncertain and vague﻿—was fostered by that profound belief in the devil and in prodigies which was yet to endure for centuries in the popular mind. The final scene of the last judgment was sculptured over the portals of every cathedral, and on entering the sanctuary of the church one passed under the balance of the archangel, on whose left writhed the bodies of the devils and the damned, delivered over to the eternal flames of hell.

But the idea that the world was to end was not confined to the Church. In the twelfth century astrologers terrified Europe by the announcement of a conjunction of all the planets in the constellation of the scales. This conjunction indeed, occurred, for on September 15th all the planets were found between the 180th and 190th degrees of longitude. But the end of the world did not come.

===Chinese===
我们今天之所以能见到那些宏伟的大教堂——它们经受了时间的摧残，激起千秋万代的惊叹——正是拜这个危急时期所赐。巨大的财富被慷慨地赐给教士，他们的财富又因捐赠和继承而增长。一个新的时代似乎即将来临。“公元 1000 年之后，”拉乌尔·格拉贝尔接着写道，“世界各地的圣殿都被彻底翻修一新，尤其是在意大利和高卢，尽管它们大部分根本无需修缮。基督教国家争相建造宏伟的教堂。整个世界仿佛被一股共同的冲动推动着，抖落昔日的破衣，换上新装；信徒们不满足于把几乎所有的主教座堂都重建一遍，还装饰了奉献给各位圣徒的修道院，甚至小村庄里的礼拜堂。”

阴沉的公元 1000 年随着消逝的诸世纪走进了过去，但教会经历了怎样动荡的时局啊！教皇们沦为相互争斗的萨克森皇帝和拉齐奥诸侯手中的傀儡。整个基督教世界刀兵四起。危机过去了，但世界末日的问题依然存在；对这一可怕事件的信仰——虽然模糊不定——却因那种对魔鬼和奇事的深信不疑而愈加深厚，这种深信还将在民众心里延续好几个世纪。最后审判的终场戏被雕在每座大教堂的门楣上；走进教堂的圣所，人们便从大天使的天平下面经过，天平的左边，魔鬼和被打入地狱者的躯体在扭曲挣扎，被交给地狱的永火。

但世界将终结的想法并不限于教会。十二世纪时，占星家们宣布所有的行星将在天秤座会合，使欧洲陷入恐慌。这次会合确实发生了：9 月 15 日，所有的行星都处在黄经 180 度至 190 度之间。但世界末日并没有来。

===Original===
The celebrated alchemist, Arnauld de Villeneuve, foretold it again for the year 1335. In 1406, under Charles VI, an eclipse of the Sun, occurring on June 16th, produced a general panic, which is chronicled by Juvénal of the Ursuline Order: “It is a pitiable sight,” he says, “to see people taking refuge in the churches as if the world were about to perish.” In 1491 St. Vincent Ferrier wrote a treatise entitled, “*De la Fin du Monde et de la Science Spirituelle.*” He allows Christendom as many years of life as there are verses in the psalter, namely, 2,537. Then a German astrologer, one Stoffler, predicted that on February 20, 1524, a general deluge would result from a conjunction of the planets. He was very generally believed, and the panic was extreme. Property situated in valleys, along river banks, or near the sea, was sold to the less credulous for a mere nothing. A certain doctor, Auriol, of Toulouse, had an ark built for himself, his family and his friends, and Bodin asserts that he was not the only one who took this precaution.

There were few sceptics. The grand chancellor of Charles V sought the advice of Pierre Martyr, who told him that the event would not be as fatal as was feared, but that the conjunction of the planets would doubtless occasion grave disasters. The fatal day arrived﻿ ﻿… and never had the month of February been so dry! But this did not prevent new predictions for the year 1532, by the astrologer of the elector of Brandenburg, Jean Carion; and again for the year 1584, by the astrologer Cyprian Lëowitz. It was again a question of a deluge, due to planetary conjunctions. “The terror of the populace,” writes a contemporary, Louis Guyon, “was extreme, and the churches could not hold the multitudes which fled to them for refuge; many made their wills without stopping to think that this availed little if the world was really to perish; others donated their goods to the clergy, in the hope that their prayers would put off the day of judgment.”

In 1588 there was another astrological prediction, couched in apocalyptic language, as follows: “The eighth year following the fifteen hundred and eightieth anniversary of the birth of Christ will be a year of prodigies and terror. If in this terrible year the globe be not dissolved in dust, and the land and the sea be not destroyed, every kingdom will be overthrown and humanity will travail in pain.”

===Chinese===
著名的炼金术士阿尔诺·德·维伦纽夫又把末日预言在 1335 年。1406 年，查理六世治下，6 月 16 日发生的一次日食引起了普遍的恐慌，乌尔苏拉会的朱弗纳尔记载了这件事：“看到人们躲进教堂，仿佛世界就要毁灭，真是一幅凄惨的景象。”1491 年，圣樊尚·费里耶写了一篇题为《论世界末日与灵性科学》的论文。他给基督教世界定的寿数，与《诗篇》的节数一样多，即 2,537 年。后来，一位德国占星家施托夫勒预言：1524 年 2 月 20 日，行星的会合将引起一场普遍的洪水。人们普遍相信他，恐慌到了极点。坐落在山谷、河岸或海滨的产业，以近乎白送的价钱卖给了那些不那么轻信的人。图卢兹的一位医生奥里奥尔给自己、家人和朋友造了一艘方舟；博丹断言，采取这种预防措施的并非他一人。

怀疑者寥寥无几。查理五世的大法官征询皮埃尔·马蒂尔的意见；马蒂尔告诉他，这件事不会像担心的那样致命，但行星的会合无疑会引起严重的灾难。那个致命的日子到来了……而那个二月，从来还没有那样干旱过！但这并不妨碍新的预言接着出现：勃兰登堡选帝侯的占星家让·卡里翁预言 1532 年，占星家齐普里安·莱奥维茨又预言 1584 年。说的还是行星会合引起的那场洪水。“民众的恐怖，”同时代人路易·吉永写道，“到了极点，教堂装不下涌去避难的人群；许多人立下遗嘱，却想不到如果世界真要毁灭，这几乎没有用处；另一些人把财产捐给教士，指望他们的祈祷能推迟审判之日。”

1588 年又出现了一个占星预言，用的是启示录式的语言：“基督诞生后一千五百八十年的第八年，将是奇事与恐怖之年。倘若在这可怕的一年里，地球不化为尘埃，陆地和海洋不遭毁灭，那么每一个王国都将倾覆，人类将在痛苦中呻吟。”

===Original===
As might be expected, the celebrated soothsayer, Nostradamus, is found among these prophets of evil. In his book of rhymed prophecies, entitled *Centuries*, we find the following quatrain, which excited much speculation:

> *Quand Georges Dieu crucifiera,*
 
 

 
 *Que Marc le ressuscitera,*
 
 

 
 *Et que St. Jean le portera,*
 
 

 
 *La fin du monde arrivera.*

===Chinese===
不出所料，著名的预言家诺查丹玛斯也在这群凶事预言者之列。在他那部题为《诸世纪》的押韵预言集里，我们看到下面这首曾引起纷纷猜测的四行诗：

> *当圣乔治将上帝钉上十字架，*



 *圣马可又将祂复活，*



 *而圣约翰将祂背负，*



 *世界末日便将来临。*


===Original===
The meaning of which is, that when Easter falls on the twenty-fifth of April (St. Mark’s day), Holy Friday will fall on the twenty-third (St. George’s day), and Corpus Christi on the twenty-fourth of June (St. John’s day), and the end of the world will come. This verse was not without malice, for at this time (Nostradamus died in 1556) the calendar had not been reformed; this was not done until 1582, and it was impossible for Easter to fall on the twenty-fifth of April. In the sixteenth century, the twenty-fifth of April corresponded to the fifteenth; the day following November 4, 1582, was called the fifteenth. After the introduction of the Gregorian calendar, Easter might fall on the twenty-fifth of April, its latest possible date, and this was the case in 1666, 1734, 1886, as it will be again in 1942, 2038, 2190, etc., the end of the world, however, not being a necessary consequence of this coincidence.

Planetary conjunctions, eclipses and comets were alike the basis for prophecies of evil. Among the comets recorded in history we may mention, as the most remarkable from this point of view, that of William the Conqueror, which appeared in 1066, and which is pictured on the tapestry of Queen Matilda, at Bayeux; that of 1264, which, it is said, disappeared the very day of the death of Pope Urban IV; that of 1327, one of the largest and most imposing ever seen, which “presaged” the death of Frederick, king of Sicily; that of 1399, which Juvénal, the Ursuline, described as “the harbinger of coming evil;” that of 1402, to which was ascribed the death of Gian Galeazzo, Visconti, duke of Milan; that of 1456, which filled all Christendom with terror, under Pope Calixtus III, during the war with the Turks, and which is associated with the history of the Angelus; and that of 1472, which preceded the death of the brother of Louis XI. There were others, also, associated like the preceding, with catastrophes and wars, and especially with the dreaded last hours of the race. That of 1527 is described by Ambroise Paré, and by Simon Goulart, as formed of severed heads, poniards and bloody clouds. The comet of 1531 was thought to herald the death of Louise of Savoy, mother of Francis I, and this princess shared the popular superstition in reference to evil stars: “Behold!” she exclaimed from her bed, on perceiving the comet through the window, “behold an omen which is not given to one of low degree. God sends it as a warning to us. Let us prepare to meet death.” Three days after, she died. But the famous comet of Charles V, appearing in 1556, was perhaps the most memorable of all. It had been identified as the comet of 1264, and its return was announced for 1848. But it did not reappear.

The comets of 1577, 1607, 1652 and 1665 were the subjects of endless commentaries, forming a library by themselves. At the last of these Alphonso VI, king of Portugal, angrily discharged his pistol, with the most grotesque defiance. Pierre Petit, by order of Louis XIV, published a work designed to counteract the foolish, and political, apprehensions excited by comets. This illustrious king desired to be without a rival, the only Sun, “*Nec pluribus impar!*” and would not admit the supposition that the glory of France could be imperilled even by a celestial phenomenon.

===Chinese===
它的意思是：当复活节落在 4 月 25 日（圣马可节）、神圣星期五落在 4 月 23 日（圣乔治节）、基督圣体节落在 6 月 24 日（圣约翰节）时，世界末日就会来临。这首诗不无恶意，因为在那个时代（诺查丹玛斯卒于 1556 年），历法尚未改革；那要等到 1582 年，而复活节根本不可能落在 4 月 25 日。在十六世纪，新历的 4 月 25 日相当于旧历的 15 日；1582 年 11 月 4 日的次日，就被叫作 15 日。采用格里高利历之后，复活节就可能落在 4 月 25 日这个最晚的可能日期上了，1666 年、1734 年、1886 年都是如此，1942 年、2038 年、2190 年等还会如此；不过，世界末日并不是这种巧合的必然结果。

行星的会合、日食和彗星，同样都是凶兆预言的依据。在历史上记载的彗星当中，从这个观点看最值得一提的有：征服者威廉的彗星，出现于 1066 年，织在巴约的马蒂尔达王后挂毯上；1264 年的彗星，据说在教皇乌尔班四世去世的当天消失；1327 年的彗星，是有史以来最大最壮观的彗星之一，它“预兆”了西西里国王腓特烈之死；1399 年的彗星，被乌尔苏拉会的朱弗纳尔描述为“将至灾祸的先驱”；1402 年的彗星，人们把米兰公爵吉安·加莱亚佐·维斯孔蒂之死归因于它；1456 年的彗星，在教皇加里斯都三世治下、与土耳其人交战之际，令整个基督教世界充满恐怖，并与三钟经的历史联系在一起；还有 1472 年的彗星，它出现在路易十一之兄去世之前。此外还有一些，与前面那些一样，同灾祸和战争、尤其同人惧怕的种族最后时辰联系在一起。1527 年的彗星，被安布鲁瓦兹·帕雷和西蒙·古拉尔描绘成由断头、匕首和血云组成。1531 年的彗星被认为是弗朗索瓦一世之母、萨伏依的路易丝去世的先兆，而这位太后本人也分享着关于凶星的民间迷信：“看哪！”她从床上望见窗外那颗彗星时喊道，“看哪，这不是赐给卑贱者的征兆。上帝派它来警告我们。让我们准备迎接死亡吧。”三天之后，她死了。但查理五世那颗著名的彗星——出现于 1556 年——或许是所有彗星中最令人难忘的。它被认作 1264 年的那颗彗星，人们预告它将在 1848 年回归。可它没有再现。

1577、1607、1652 和 1665 年的彗星，是无数评论的主题，单凭它们便可自成一个书库。对最后一颗，葡萄牙国王阿方索六世怒气冲冲地开了一枪，摆出最滑稽的挑衅姿态。皮埃尔·珀蒂奉路易十四之命，出版了一部著作，旨在消除彗星引起的愚蠢而带政治性的恐慌。这位显赫的君主不愿有任何对手，要做唯一的太阳——“*Nec pluribus impar!*”（纵与群日争辉亦不让）——他不肯承认，法兰西的荣耀竟会受到哪怕一种天象的威胁。

===Original===
One of the greatest comets which ever struck the imagination of men was assuredly the famous comet of 1680, to which Newton devoted so much attention. “It issued,” said Lemonnier, “with a frightful velocity from the depths of space and seemed falling directly into the Sun and was seen to vanish with an equal velocity. It was visible for four months. It approached quite near to the Earth, and Whiston ascribed the deluge to its former appearance.” Bayle wrote a treatise to prove the absurdity of beliefs founded on these portents. Madame de Sévigné writing to her cousin, Count de Bussy-Rabutin, says: “We have a comet of enormous size; its tail is the most beautiful object conceivable. Every person of note is alarmed and believes that heaven, interested in their fate, sends them a warning in this comet. They say that the courtiers of Cardinal Mazarin, who is despaired of by his physicians believe this prodigy is in honor of his passing away, and tell him of the terror with which it has inspired them. He had the sense to laugh at them, and to reply facetiously that the comet did him too much honor. In truth we ought all to agree with him, for human pride assumes too much when it believes that death is attended by such signs from heaven.”

We see that comets were gradually losing their prestige. Yet we read in a treatise of the astronomer Bernouilli this singular remark: “If the head of the comet be not a visible sign of the anger of God, *the tail may well be*.”

Fear of the end of the world was reawakened by the appearance of comets in 1773; a great panic spread throughout Europe, and Paris itself was alarmed. Here is an extract from the memoirs of Bachaumont, accessible to every reader:

===Chinese===
曾深深打动人类想象的最大彗星之一，无疑要数 1680 年那颗著名的彗星，牛顿曾对它倾注大量心血。“它以骇人的速度从太空深处冲出，”勒莫尼耶说，“仿佛径直坠向太阳，随后又以同样的速度消失不见。它可见了四个月。它相当接近地球，而惠斯顿把大洪水归因于它上一次的出现。”培尔写了一篇论文，证明建立在这些凶兆之上的信仰何等荒谬。塞维涅夫人在写给堂兄比西-拉比丹伯爵的信里说：“我们有一颗大得惊人的彗星；它的尾巴是所能想象的最美的东西。每一位要人都惊惶不安，相信上天关切他们的命运，特派这颗彗星来示警。他们说，马扎然红衣主教——医生们已经对他不抱希望——的廷臣们相信，这一奇象是为了恭送他谢幕，还向他讲起它使他们感到的恐怖。他有这份见识，对之一笑，还风趣地回答说，这颗彗星太抬举他了。说真的，我们都该同意他的看法，因为人的骄傲未免太自以为是，竟以为死亡还会有上天这样的征象相伴。”

我们看到，彗星正渐渐失去它们的威望。然而，在天文学家贝尔努利的一部论文里，我们读到这样一句奇语：“倘若彗星的头不是上帝愤怒的可见标志，*那么尾巴很可能是*。”

1773 年彗星的出现，重新唤起了对世界末日的恐惧；一场大恐慌席卷欧洲，连巴黎也为之震动。这里从巴赫蒙的《回忆录》里摘引一段，每位读者都可以查到：

===Original===
“May 6th, 1773. In the last public meeting of the Academy of Sciences, M. de Lalande was to read by far the most interesting paper of all; this, however, he was not able to do, for lack of time. It concerned the comets which, by approaching the Earth, may cause revolutions, and dealt especially with that one whose return is expected in eighteen years. But although he affirmed that it was not one of those which would harm the Earth, and that, moreover, he had observed that one could not fix, with any exactness, the order of such occurrences, there exists, nevertheless, a very general anxiety.

“May 9th. The cabinet of M. de Lalande is filled with the curious who come to question him concerning the above memoir, and, in order to reassure those who have been alarmed by the exaggerated rumors circulated about it, he will doubtless be forced to make it public. The excitement has been so great that some ignorant fanatics have besought the archbishop to institute prayers for forty hours, in order to avert the deluge which menaces us; and this prelate would have authorized these prayers, had not the Academy shown him the ridicule which such a step would produce.

“May 14th. The memoir of M. de Lalande has appeared. He says that it is his opinion that, of the sixty known comets, eight, by their near approach to the Earth, might produce a pressure such that the sea would leave its bed and cover a part of the world.”

===Chinese===
“1773 年 5 月 6 日。在科学院最近一次公开会议上，德·拉朗德先生本要宣读所有论文中最引人入胜的一篇，却因时间不够而未能如愿。论文讨论的是那些因接近地球而可能引起剧变的彗星，尤其着重于预计十八年后回归的那一颗。虽然他申明它不属于会伤害地球的那一类彗星，而且他发现自己无法以任何精确性确定这类事件的次序，然而一种非常普遍的不安已经存在。

“5 月 9 日。德·拉朗德先生的会客室里挤满了好奇的人，前来询问上述论文的情况；为了安抚那些被夸大传闻惊扰的人，他恐怕不得不把论文公之于众。骚动如此之大，一些无知的狂热者竟恳求大主教设立四十小时祈祷，以求躲避威胁我们的洪水；若不是科学院让他看清此举会招来的讥笑，这位高级教士本来是会批准这些祈祷的。

“5 月 14 日。德·拉朗德先生的论文发表了。他说，依他的意见，在已知六十颗彗星中，有八颗因贴近地球，可能产生一种足以使海水离开海床、淹没世界一部分的压力。”

===Original===
In time, the excitement died away. The fear of comets assumed a new form. They were no longer regarded as indications of the anger of God, but their collision with the Earth was discussed from a scientific point of view, and these collisions were not considered free of danger. At the close of the last century, Laplace stated his views on this question, in the forcible language which we have quoted in Chapter II.

In this century, predictions concerning the end of the world have several times been associated with the appearance of comets. It was announced that the comet of Biela, for example, would intersect the Earth’s orbit on October 29, 1832, which it did, as predicted. There was great excitement. Once more the end of things was declared at hand. Humanity was threatened. What was going to happen?

The orbit, that is to say the path, of the Earth had been confounded with the Earth itself. The latter was not to reach that point of its orbit traversed by the comet until November 30th, more than a month after the comet’s passage, and the latter was at no time to be within 20,000,000 leagues of us. Once more we got off with a fright.

===Chinese===
随着时间推移，骚动平息了。对彗星的恐惧采取了一种新形式。它们不再被视为上帝愤怒的表征；人们从科学的观点讨论它们与地球的相撞，而且并不认为这些相撞全无危险。上世纪末，拉普拉斯用我们在第二章引用过的那种有力的语言，陈述了他对这个问题的看法。

在本世纪，关于世界末日的预言曾多次与彗星的出现联系在一起。比如有人宣布，比拉彗星将在 1832 年 10 月 29 日与地球轨道相交，它也确实如预言的那样穿过了。一时群情激奋。万物之终再一次被宣告在即。人类受到了威胁。将会发生什么事呢？

地球的轨道，也就是它的路径，被同地球本身混为一谈了。地球要到 11 月 30 日才会到达彗星所穿过的那个轨道点，比彗星过境晚了一个多月，而且彗星与我们相距从不曾少于 20,000,000 里格。我们又一次只是虚惊一场。

===Original===
It was the same in 1857. Some prophet of ill omen had declared that the famous comet of Charles V, whose periodic time was thought to be three centuries, would return on the 13th of June of that year. More than one timid soul was rendered anxious, and the confessionals of Paris were more than usually crowded with penitents. Another prediction was made public in 1872, in the name of an astronomer, who, however, was not responsible for it﻿—M. Plantamour, director of the Geneva observatory.

As in the case of comets, so with other unusual phenomena, such as total solar eclipses, mysterious suns appearing suddenly in the skies, showers of shooting stars, great volcanic eruptions accompanied with the darkness of night and seeming to threaten the burial of the world in ashes, earthquakes overthrowing and engulfing houses and cities﻿—all these grand and terrible events have been connected with the fear of an immediate and universal end of men and things.

The history of eclipses alone would suffice to fill a volume, no less interesting than the history of comets. Confining our attention to a modern example, one of the last total eclipses of the Sun, visible in France, that of August 12, 1654, had been foretold by astronomers, and its announcement had produced great alarm. For some it meant the overthrow of states and the fall of Rome; for others it signified a new deluge; there were those who believed that nothing less than the destruction of the world by fire was inevitable; while the more collected anticipated the poisoning of the atmosphere. Belief in these dreaded results were so widespread, that, in order to escape them, and by the express order of physicians, many terrified people shut themselves up in closed cellars, warmed and perfumed. We refer the reader, especially, to the second evening of *Les Mondes* of Fontenelle. Another writer of the same century, Petit, to whom we referred a moment ago, in his *Dissertation on the Nature of Comets* says, that the consternation steadily increased up to the fatal day, and that a country curate, unable to confess all who believed their last hour was at hand, at sermon time told his parishioners not to be in such haste, for the eclipse had been put off for a fortnight; and these good people were as ready to believe in the postponement of the eclipse as they had been in its malign influence.

===Chinese===
1857 年也是这样。某个不祥的预言家宣称，那颗周期被认定为三个世纪的查理五世著名彗星，将在那年的 6 月 13 日回归。不止一颗怯懦的心灵为之焦虑，巴黎的忏悔室里挤满了前所未有的忏悔者。1872 年，又公开了另一则以一位天文学家名义发布的预言——不过他本人对此并不负责——那就是日内瓦天文台台长普朗塔穆尔先生。

正如对彗星一样，对其他异常现象也是如此：日全食、天空中骤然出现的神秘太阳、流星雨、伴随着暗夜、似乎要用灰烬埋葬世界的大火山喷发、掀翻并吞没房屋与城市的地震——所有这些宏大而可怕的事件，都曾与对人类和万物即将普遍终结的恐惧联系在一起。

单是日食的历史就足以写满一卷，其引人入胜不亚于彗星的历史。只举一个近代的例子：法国可见的最近几次日全食之一，1654 年 8 月 12 日的那次，已被天文学家预告，消息一出便引起了极大的恐慌。对某些人，它意味着国家的倾覆和罗马的陷落；对另一些人，它预示一场新的洪水；还有人相信，世界毁于大火已是不可避免；而比较镇定的人则担心大气中毒。对这些可怕后果的信仰流传如此之广，以致许多吓坏了的人遵照医生的明确嘱咐，把自己关在烧得暖暖的、熏过香的密闭地窖里躲避。我们特别请读者参看丰特奈勒《多元世界》的第二夜。同一世纪的另一位作家，就是方才提到的那位珀蒂，在他的《论彗星的性质》里说，惊惶在致命之日到来之前有增无已；有位乡村本堂神父，面对众多自认死期已至、无法一一听取忏悔的信徒，在讲道时告诉他们不必如此着急，因为日食已经推迟半个月了；而这些善良的人相信日食的凶险有多快，相信日食的推迟就有多快。

===Original===
At the time of the last total solar eclipses visible in France, namely, those of May 12, 1706; May 22, 1724, and July 8, 1842, as also of the partial ones of October 9, 1847; July 28, 1851; March 15, 1858; July 18, 1860, and December 22, 1870, there was more or less apprehension on the part of the timid; at least, we know, from trustworthy sources, that in each of these cases these natural phenomena were interpreted by a certain class in Europe as possible signs of divine wrath, and in several religious educational establishments the pupils were requested to offer up prayers as the time of the eclipse drew near. This mystical interpretation of the order of nature is slowly disappearing among enlightened nations, and the next total eclipse of the Sun, visible in southern France on May 28, 1900, will probably inspire no fear on the French side of the Pyrenees; but it might be premature to make the same statement regarding those who will observe it from the Spanish side of the mountains.

Among uncivilized people these phenomena excite today the same terror which they once did among us. This fact is frequently attested by travellers, especially in Africa. During the eclipse of July 18, 1860, in Algeria, men and women resorted to prayer or fled affrighted to their homes. During the eclipse of July 29, 1878, which was total in the United States, a negro, suddenly crazed with terror, and persuaded that the end of the world was coming, cut the throats of his wife and children.

It must be admitted that such phenomena are well calculated to overwhelm the imagination. The Sun, the god of day, the star upon whose light we are dependent, grows dim; and, just before it becomes extinguished, takes on a sickly and mournful hue. The light of the sky pales, the animal creation is stricken with terror, the beast of burden falters at his task, the dog flees to its master, the hen retreats with her brood to the coop, the birds cease their songs, and have been seen even to drop dead with fright. Arago relates that during the total eclipse of the Sun at Perpignan, on July 8, 1842, twenty thousand spectators were assembled, forming an impressive spectacle. “When the solar disc was nearly obscured, an irresistible anxiety took possession of everybody; each felt the need of sharing his impressions with his neighbor. A deep murmur arose, like that of the far away sea after a storm. This murmur deepened as the crescent of light grew less, and when it had disappeared and sudden darkness had supervened, the silence which ensued marked this phase of the eclipse as accurately as the pendulum of our astronomical clock. The magnificence of the spectacle triumphed over the petulance of youth, over the frivolity which some people mistake for a sign of superiority, over the indifference which the soldier frequently assumes. A profound silence reigned also in the sky: the birds had ceased their songs. After a solemn interval of about two minutes, joyous transports and frantic applause greeted with the same spontaneity the first reappearance of the solar rays, and the melancholy and indefinable sense of depression gave way to a deep and unfeigned exultation which no one sought to moderate or repress.”

===Chinese===
在法国可见的最近几次日全食之时——即 1706 年 5 月 12 日、1724 年 5 月 22 日和 1842 年 7 月 8 日——以及 1847 年 10 月 9 日、1851 年 7 月 28 日、1858 年 3 月 15 日、1860 年 7 月 18 日和 1870 年 12 月 22 日这几次偏食之时，怯懦的人们或多或少都感到不安；至少我们从可靠的来源得知，在每一种情况下，这些自然现象都被欧洲的某一类人解释为神怒的可能征兆，而且在好几所教会学校里，当日食临近时，学生们被要求献上祈祷。对自然秩序的这种神秘解释，正在开化民族中慢慢消失；1900 年 5 月 28 日在法国南部可见的下一次日全食，大概不会在比利牛斯山的法国一侧引起恐惧；但对从山脉西班牙一侧观测它的人作同样的断言，恐怕就为时过早了。

在未开化的民族中，这些现象今天仍会引起它们从前在我们中间引起过的那种恐怖。旅行者们经常证实这一点，在非洲尤其如此。1860 年 7 月 18 日日食期间，在阿尔及利亚，男男女女或祈祷，或惊逃回家。1878 年 7 月 29 日在美国发生的日全食期间，一个被恐怖突然逼疯的黑人，确信世界末日将至，割断了他妻子和孩子们的喉咙。

必须承认，这类现象极足以压倒人的想象。太阳，白日之神，我们的光赖以为生的那颗恒星，黯淡了下去；而就在熄灭之前，它染上一种病态而悲凉的颜色。天光变得苍白，动物界陷入惊恐，役畜在活计前踉跄，狗逃向主人，母鸡带着一窝小鸡退回鸡舍，鸟儿停止了歌唱，甚至有人见过它们吓得坠地而死。阿拉戈讲述说，1842 年 7 月 8 日在佩皮尼昂观测日全食时，聚集了两万名观众，构成一幅动人的景象。“当日轮几乎被遮尽时，一种不可抗拒的焦灼攫住了每一个人；人人都感到需要与邻人分享自己的印象。一阵深沉的低语升起，像风暴过后遥远的海。随着光亮的月牙越来越小，这低语越来越深；当月牙消失、骤然的黑暗降临时，随之而来的寂静，像我们天文钟的钟摆一样准确地标示出日食的这一时刻。壮丽的景象战胜了年轻人的浮躁，战胜了被某些人误作优越感标志的轻浮，战胜了士兵惯常装出的漠然。天上也笼罩着一片深沉的寂静：鸟儿停止了歌唱。在约两分钟庄严的间歇之后，最初的太阳光芒重新露头，欢腾的狂喜和热烈的掌声以同样的自发性随之而起；那种忧郁而难以名状的压抑感，让位于一种深沉而真诚的欢庆，谁也不想加以节制或抑制。”

===Original===
Everyone who witnessed this phenomenon, one of the most sublime which nature offers, was profoundly moved, and took away with him an impression never to be forgotten. The peasants especially were terrified by the darkness, as they believed that they were losing their sight. A poor child, tending his flock, completely ignorant of what was coming, saw the Sun slowly growing dim in a cloudless sky. When its light had entirely disappeared the poor child, completely carried away by terror, began to cry and call for help. His tears flowed again when the first ray of light reappeared. Reassured, he clasped his hands, crying, “O, beautiful sun!”

Is not the cry of this child the cry of humanity?

So long as eclipses were not known to be the natural consequences of the motion of the Moon about the Earth, and before it was understood that their occurrence could be predicted with the utmost precision, it was natural that they should have produced a deep impression and been associated with the idea of the end of the world. The same is true of other celestial phenomena and notably of the sudden appearance of unknown suns, an event much rarer than an eclipse.

===Chinese===
凡目睹过这一现象——自然所提供的最壮丽的现象之一——的人，无不深受震动，并带走一幅终生难忘的印象。农民们尤其被黑暗吓坏了，以为自己正在失明。一个放羊的穷孩子，对将要发生的事一无所知，看见太阳在无云的天空中渐渐黯淡。当它的光完全消失时，这可怜的孩子被恐怖整个吞没，哭喊起来，呼喊救命。当第一缕光重新出现时，他又流泪了。放下心来的他合起双手，喊道：“啊，美丽的太阳！”

这孩子的呼喊，不正是人类的呼喊吗？

只要日食还不为人所知是月球绕地球运动的自然结果，只要人们还不理解它的发生可以以最高的精度预告，那么日食引起深刻的印象、并与世界末日的观念联系在一起，便是很自然的事。其他天象也是如此，尤其是未知太阳的骤然出现——这是比日食罕见得多的事件。

===Original===
The most celebrated of these appearances was that of 1572. On the 11th of November of that year, about a month after the massacre of St. Bartholomew, a brilliant star of the first magnitude suddenly appeared in the constellation of Cassiopeia. The stupefaction was general, not only on the part of the public, to which it was visible every night in the sky, but also on the part of scientists, who could not explain its appearance. Astrologers found a solution of the enigma in the assertion that it was the star of the Magi, whose reappearance announced the return of the Son of God, the last judgment and the resurrection. This statement made a deep impression upon all classes of society. The star gradually diminished in splendor, and at the end of about eighteen months went out, without having caused any other disaster than that which human folly itself adds to the misery of a none too prosperous planet. Science records several apparitions of this nature, but the above was the most remarkable. A like agitation has accompanied all the grand phenomena of nature, especially those which have been unforeseen. In the chronicles of the middle ages, and even in more recent memoirs, we read of the terror which the aurora borealis, showers of shooting stars and the fall of meteorites have produced among the alarmed spectators. Recently, during the meteor shower of November 27, 1872, when the sky was filled with more than forty thousand meteorites belonging to the dispersed comet of Biela, women of the lower classes, at Nice especially, as also at Rome, in their excitement sought information of those whom they thought able to explain the cause of these celestial fireworks, which they had at once associated with the end of the world and with the fall of the stars, which it was foretold would usher in that last great event.

Earthquakes and volcanic eruptions have sometimes attained such proportions as to lead to the fear that the end of the world was at hand. Imagine the state of mind of the inhabitants of Herculaneum and of Pompeii when the eruption of Vesuvius buried them in showers of ashes! Was not this for them the end of the world? And more recently, were not those who witnessed the eruption of Krakatoa of the same opinion? Impenetrable darkness lasting eighteen hours, an atmosphere like a furnace, filling the eyes, nose and ears with ashes, the deep and incessant cannonade of the volcano, the falling of pumice stones from the black sky, the terrible scene illuminated only at intervals by the lurid lightning or the fireballs on the spars and rigging of vessels, the thunder echoing from cloud and sea with an infernal musketry, the shower of ashes turning into a deluge of mud﻿—this was the experience of the passengers of a Java vessel during the night of eighteen hours, from the 26th to the 28th of August, 1883, when a portion of the island of Krakatoa was hurled into the air, and the sea, after having first retreated, swept upon the shore to a height of thirty-five meters and to a distance of from one to ten kilometers over a coastline of five hundred kilometers, and in the reflux carried away with it the four cities, Tjiringin, Mérak, Telok-Bétong and Anjer, and the entire population of the region, more than forty thousand souls. For a long time the progress of vessels was hindered by floating bodies inextricably interlaced; and human fingers, with their nails, and fragments of heads, with their hair were found in the stomachs of fishes. Those who escaped, or who saw the catastrophe from some vessel, and lived to welcome again the light of day, which had seemed forever extinguished, relate in terror with what resignation they expected the end of the world, persuaded that its very foundations were giving way and that the knell of a universal doom had sounded. One eyewitness assures us that he would not again pass through such an experience for all the wealth that could be imagined. The Sun was extinguished and death seemed to reign sovereign over nature. This eruption, moreover, was of such terrific violence that it was heard through the Earth at the antipodes; it reached an altitude of twenty thousand meters, producing an atmospheric disturbance which made the circuit of the entire globe in thirty-five hours (the barometer fell four millimeters in Paris even), and left for more than a year in the upper layers of the atmosphere a fine dust, which, illumined by the Sun, gave rise to those magnificent twilight displays admired so much throughout the world.

These are formidable disturbances, partial ends of the world. Certain earthquakes deserve citation with these terrible volcanic eruptions, so disastrous have been their consequences. In the Earthquake of Lisbon, November 1, 1755, thirty thousand persons perished; the shock was felt over an area four times as large as that of Europe. When Lima was destroyed, October 28, 1724, the sea rose twenty-seven meters above its ordinary level, rushed upon the city and erased it so completely that not a single house was left. Vessels were found in the fields several kilometers from the shore. On December 10, 1869, the inhabitants of the city of Onlah, in Asia Minor, alarmed by subterranean noises and a first violent trembling of the Earth, took refuge on a neighboring hilltop, whence, to their stupefaction, they saw several crevasses open in the city which within a few moments entirely disappeared in the bowels of the Earth. We have direct evidence that under circumstances far less dramatic, as for example on the occasion of the Earthquake at Nice, February 23, 1887, the idea of the end of the world was the very first which presented itself to the mind.

===Chinese===
这类出现中最著名的一次发生在 1572 年。那年 11 月 11 日，圣巴托罗缪大屠杀之后约一个月，一颗明亮的一等星突然出现在仙后座。惊愕是普遍的：不只是夜夜在天上望见它的公众，连无法解释其出现的科学家也同样惊愕。占星家们在这样的断言中找到了谜底：那是东方三博士的星，它的重现宣告上帝之子的复临、最后的审判和复活。这一说法给社会各阶层都留下了深刻的印象。那颗星的光辉逐渐减弱，约十八个月后熄灭了；除了人类愚蠢本身给这颗不算太富足的行星添加的苦难之外，它没有造成任何别的灾难。科学记录过几次这类出现，但上述一次最为引人注目。一切宏大的自然现象，尤其是那些未曾预料的，都伴随着类似的骚动。在中世纪的编年史里，甚至在更晚近的回忆录里，我们读到北极光、流星雨和陨石坠落使惊恐的目击者产生的恐怖。就在不久以前，1872 年 11 月 27 日的流星雨中——当时天上落下了属于解体的比拉彗星的四万多颗流星——尼斯、尤其是罗马的下层妇女们在激动之下，向她们认为能够解释这些天界焰火成因的人打听消息；她们当即把它同世界末日、同众星的坠落联系在一起，因为据预言，那将引出最后的大事。

地震和火山喷发有时达到如此规模，以致人们担心世界末日已到。试想赫库兰尼姆和庞贝的居民，当维苏威火山的喷发把他们埋在灰雨之下时，心里是什么滋味！对他们来说，这不就是世界末日吗？再说晚近一些，目睹喀拉喀托火山喷发的人，难道不也是这样想的吗？持续十八小时的不透光的黑暗，像炉膛一样的大气，使眼睛、鼻子和耳朵塞满灰烬，火山深沉而不间断的炮轰，浮石从黑色的天空坠落，那可怕的场面只有时而借着船桅和索具上闪过的闪电或火球才被照亮，雷声挟着地狱般的枪炮声在云与海之间回荡，灰雨化为泥泞的豪雨——这是一艘爪哇船只上的乘客们在 1883 年 8 月 26 日至 28 日那个十八小时的黑夜里的经历；那一夜，喀拉喀托岛的一部分被抛上了天，海水先是退去，继而以三十五米的高度、一至十公里的纵深，扑向五百公里长的海岸线，又在回卷中把四座城市——奇林京、默拉克、直落勿洞和安杰尔——连同该地区的全部人口、四万多条性命一并卷走。很长时间里，船只的行进被纠结缠绕的漂浮尸体所阻；在鱼腹中发现了带着指甲的人的手指，和带着头发的头颅碎片。那些死里逃生的人，或从某艘船上目睹这场灾难、又活着迎来那似乎永远熄灭了的白昼之光的人，惊恐地讲述他们怀着怎样的听天由命等待世界末日，确信世界的根基正在崩塌，普遍覆灭的丧钟已经敲响。一位目击者向我们保证，纵使把可以想象的一切财富都给他，他也不愿再经历一次这样的事。太阳熄灭了，死亡仿佛君临着自然。况且，这次喷发的猛烈程度如此可怕，连地球的对跖点都听得见它；它冲上两万米的高空，造成的大气扰动以三十五小时绕行全球一周（连巴黎的气压表都下降了四毫米），并在大气上层留下细尘达一年多之久——那尘埃被太阳照亮，便形成了全世界赞叹不已的那些绚丽的暮霞。

这些都是可怕的大扰动，是局部的世界末日。某些地震也值得与这些可怕的火山喷发相提并论，因为它们的后果是那样惨重。1755 年 11 月 1 日的里斯本大地震，三万人丧生；震动波及的范围四倍于欧洲的面积。1724 年 10 月 28 日利马被毁时，海面比平常水位上涨了二十七米，扑向这座城市，把它抹得干干净净，连一座房子也没剩下。人们在离岸几公里的田野里发现船只。1869 年 12 月 10 日，小亚细亚翁拉赫城的居民被地下声响和第一次剧烈的震动所惊吓，躲到附近的山顶上，惊愕地看见城里裂开好几道口子，顷刻之间，整座城市消失在大地的腹中。我们有直接的证据表明，即便在远不如这般惊心动魄的情形下——例如 1887 年 2 月 23 日尼斯地震之时——世界末日的念头也是最先浮上心头的念头。

===Original===
The history of the Earth furnishes a remarkable number of like dramas, catastrophes of a partial character, threatening the world’s final destruction. It is fitting that we should devote a moment to the consideration of these great phenomena, as also to the history of that belief in the end of the world which has appeared in every age, though modified by the progress of human knowledge. Faith has in part disappeared; mystery and superstition, which struck the imagination of our ancestors, and which has been so curiously represented in the portals of our great cathedrals, and in the sculpture and painting inspired by Christian traditions, this theological aspect of the last great day, has given place to the scientific study of the probable life of the solar system to which we belong. The geocentric and anthropocentric conception of the universe, which makes man the center and end of creation, has become gradually transformed and has at last disappeared; for we know that our humble planet is but an island in the infinite, that human history has thus far been founded on pure illusions, and that the dignity of man consists in his intellectual and moral worth. Is not the destiny and sovereign end of the human mind the exact knowledge of things, the search after truth?

During the nineteenth century, evil prophets, more or less sincere, have twenty-five times announced the end of the world, basing their prophecies upon cabalistic calculations destitute of serious foundation. Like predictions will recur so long as the race exists.

But this historic interlude, although opportune, has for a moment interrupted our narrative. Let us hasten to return to the twenty-fifth century, for we have reached its most critical moment.

===Chinese===
地球的历史提供了数量可观的一类戏剧，一个个局部性质的灾变，威胁着世界的最终毁灭。我们应该花一点时间来考察这些宏大的现象，也考察那个每个时代都曾出现、只是随人类知识的进步而改头换面的世界末日信仰。信仰已部分消失；那些曾打动我们祖先想象力的神秘与迷信——它们如此奇特地表现在我们大教堂的门楣上，表现在受基督教传统启发的雕塑和绘画之中——最后审判之日的这一神学面向，已让位于对我们所属太阳系可能寿命的科学研究。那种以人为创世中心与目的的地心说和人类中心说的宇宙观，已经逐渐转变，终至消失；因为我们知道，我们这颗卑微的行星只是无限中的一个岛屿，人类历史迄今为止建立在纯粹的幻象之上，而人的尊严在于他理智与道德的价值。人类心智的使命与至高目的，难道不正是对事物的确切认识、对真理的追求吗？

在十九世纪，那些或真或假的凶事预言家，曾二十五次宣布世界末日，其预言建立在没有任何严肃根据的神秘主义计算之上。只要人类还存在，类似的预言就会一再出现。

但是这段历史的插叙虽然来得凑巧，却一时打断了我们的叙述。让我们赶快回到二十五世纪去吧，因为我们已经到了它最危急的关头。

### VII / VII

===Original===
Inexorably, with a fatality no power could arrest, like a projectile speeding from the mouth of a cannon toward the target, the comet continued to advance, following its appointed path, and hurrying, with an ever-increasing velocity, toward the point in space at which the Earth would be found on the night of July 14﻿–﻿15. The final calculations were absolutely without error. These two heavenly bodies﻿—the Earth and the comet﻿—were to meet like two trains, rushing headlong upon each other, with resistless momentum, as if impelled to mutual destruction by an insatiable rage. But in the present instance the velocity of shock would be 865 times that of two express trains having each a speed of one hundred kilometers per hour.

During the night of July 13﻿–﻿14, the comet spread over nearly the entire sky, and whirlwinds of fire could be seen by the naked eye, eddying about an axis oblique to the zenith. The appearance was that of an army of flaming meteors, in whose midst the flashing lightning produced the effect of a furious combat. The burning star had a revolution of its own, and seemed to be convulsed with pain, like a living thing. Immense jets of flame issued from various centers, some of a greenish hue, others red as blood, while the most brilliant were of a dazzling whiteness. It was evident that the Sun was acting powerfully upon this whirlpool of gases, decomposing certain of them, forming detonating compounds, electrifying the nearer portions, and repelling the smoke from about the immense nucleus which was bearing down upon the world. The comet itself emitted a light far different from the sunlight reflected by the enveloping vapors; and its flames, shooting forth in ever-increasing volume, gave it the appearance of a monster, precipitating itself upon the Earth to devour it. Perhaps the most striking feature of this spectacle was the absence of all sound. At Paris, as elsewhere, during that eventful night, the crowd instinctively maintained silence, spellbound by an indescribable fascination, endeavoring to catch some echo of the celestial thunder﻿—but not a sound was heard.

===Chinese===
彗星势不可挡地前进着，带着一种任何力量都无法遏止的宿命，像一颗刚出炮口、直扑靶心的弹丸，沿着注定的路径，以不断加快的速度，奔向太空中的那一点——7月14至15日的夜里，地球必将抵达的那一点。最后的计算绝对无误。这两个天体——地球与彗星——将像两列火车一样相会，彼此亡命地对冲，带着不可抗拒的冲量，仿佛被一种不知餍足的狂怒驱向同归于尽。只不过这一次，撞击的速度将是两列时速一百公里的特别快车的865倍。

7月13至14日夜里，彗星铺满了几乎整个天空，肉眼便能看到一个个火的旋涡，绕着一根斜指天顶的轴回旋。那景象像一支燃烧的流星大军，大军中间闪电穿梭，造成一场酣战的效果。这颗燃烧的星有它自己的旋转，仿佛因痛苦而抽搐，像一头活物。巨大的火舌从各个中心喷出，有的泛着绿光，有的红得像血，最亮的则是耀目的白。显然，太阳正在猛烈地作用于这个气体的漩涡：分解其中某些气体，生成会爆鸣的化合物，使靠近的部分带电，把浓烟从那压向世界的巨大彗核周围驱开。彗星自身发出的光，与包裹它的蒸气所反射的日光截然不同；它的火焰喷涌得越来越浩大，使它像一头扑向地球、要将它吞下肚去的巨怪。这景象最骇人之处，也许是一切声响的阙如。在巴黎，也如在别处一样，在那个重大的夜晚，人群本能地保持着沉默，被一种难以名状的慑服钉在原地，竭力想捕捉一声天雷的回响——却什么也听不见。

===Original===
The moon rose full, showing green upon the fiery background of the sky, but without brilliancy and casting no shadows. The night was no more night, for the stars had disappeared, and the sky glowed with an intense light.

The comet was approaching the Earth with a velocity of 41,000 meters per second, or 2460 kilometers per minute, that is, 147,600 kilometers per hour; and the Earth was itself travelling through space, from west to east, at the rate of 29,000 meters per second, 1740 kilometers per minute, or 104,400 kilometers per hour, in a direction oblique to the orbit of the comet, which for any meridian appeared at midnight in the northeast. Thus, in virtue of their velocities, these two celestial bodies were nearing each other at the rate of 173,000 kilometers per hour. When observation, which was in entire accord with the computations previously made, established the fact that the nucleus of the comet was at a distance no greater than that of the Moon, everyone knew that two hours later the first phenomena of the coming shock would begin.

===Chinese===
满月升起来了，在烈火般的天幕上泛着绿色，却没有光辉，也不投下影子。夜已不成其为夜：星星都已隐去，天空炽燃着一片强烈的光。

彗星正以每秒41,000米、即每分钟2460公里、也就是每小时147,600公里的速度逼近地球；而地球自己也正以每秒29,000米、每分钟1740公里、即每小时104,400公里的速度，自西向东穿越太空，方向与彗星轨道偏斜相交——在任何一条经线上，彗星都于午夜出现在东北方。于是，凭着两者的速度，这两个天体正以每小时173,000公里的速率彼此逼近。当观测——与先前所作的计算完全吻合——确立彗核的距离已不超过月球的距离时，谁都知道：两小时后，来临的冲击最初的种种现象就要开始了。

===Original===
Contrary to all expectation, Friday and Saturday, the 13th and 14th of July were, like the preceding days, wonderfully beautiful; the sun shone in a cloudless sky, the air was tranquil, the temperature rather high, but cooled by a light, refreshing breeze. Nature was in a joyous mood, the country was luxuriant with beauty, the streams murmured in the valleys, the birds sang in the woods; but the dwelling places of man were heartrendingly sad. Humanity was prostrated with terror, and the impassible calm of nature stood over against the agonizing fear of the human heart in painful and harrowing contrast.

Two millions of people had fled to Australia from Paris, London, Vienna, Berlin, St. Petersburg, Rome and Madrid. As the day of collision approached, the Transatlantic Navigation Company had been obliged to increase threefold, fourfold, and even tenfold, the number of airships, which settled like flocks of birds upon San Francisco, Honolulu, Noumea, and the Australian cities of Melbourne, Sydney and Pax. But this exodus of millions represented only the fortunate minority, and their absence was scarcely noticed in the towns and villages, swarming with restless and anxious life.

Haunted by the fear of unknown perils, for several nights no one had been able to close their eyes, or even dared to go to bed. To do so, seemed to court the last sleep and to abandon all hope of awakening again. Every face was livid with terror, every eye was sunken; the hair was dishevelled, the countenance haggard and stamped with the impress of the most frightful anguish which had ever preyed upon the human soul.

===Chinese===
出乎一切意料，7月13日星期五和14日星期六，同前几天一样，晴美得出奇；太阳照耀着无云的天空，空气宁静，气温偏高，却有一阵清爽的微风加以调剂。大自然一派欢容：乡间繁丽如绣，溪流在山谷里低语，鸟雀在林间歌唱；而人的居所却凄恻欲绝。人类被恐怖压垮了；大自然无动于衷的平静，与人心灼痛的恐惧相对而立，构成一种刺人心魄、令人肝肠寸断的对照。

已有两百万人从巴黎、伦敦、维也纳、柏林、圣彼得堡、罗马和马德里逃往澳大利亚。随着相撞之日的临近，跨大西洋航运公司（Transatlantic Navigation Company）不得不把飞艇的班次增至三倍、四倍乃至十倍；它们像一群群候鸟，降落在旧金山、檀香山、努美阿，以及澳大利亚的墨尔本（Melbourne）、悉尼（Sydney）和帕克斯（Pax）诸城。但这数百万人的大出走只代表幸运的少数；他们的离去，在挤满焦灼不安的人众、闹哄哄的城市和村镇里，几乎没有被察觉。

被未知危险的恐惧缠住，一连几夜没有人能合眼，甚至不敢上床。上床，仿佛就是去赴那最后的一睡，就是放弃再度醒来的希望。每张脸都吓得发青，每双眼睛都深陷；头发散乱，面容枯槁，烙着从未啮噬过人类灵魂的最可怕的苦痛的印记。

===Original===
The atmosphere was growing drier and warmer. Since the evening before, no one had bethought himself of food, and the stomach, usually so imperious in its demands, craved for nothing. A burning thirst was the first physiological effect of the dryness of the atmosphere, and the most self-restrained sought, in every possible way, to quench it, though without success. Physical pain had begun its work, and was soon to dominate mental suffering. Hour by hour, respiration became more difficult, more exhausting and more painful. Little children, in the presence of this new suffering, appealed in tears to their mothers.
